2. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment—oneTROY Autism Programming 
Tonight, as our Mission Moment, we would like to highlight some of the 
wonderful programs in our district that focus on autism – especially since  April 
is designated as Autism Awareness Month.  
Autism is a condition that is characterized by difficulty in communicating and 
forming relationships. Students with autism are challenged in their ability to 
recognize and understand thoughts, beliefs, desires and intentions of other 
people in order to make sense of their behavior and predict what they are going 
to do.  
To celebrate the work that we are doing in Troy Schools, specifically around 
autism, we wanted to give you the opportunity to take a peek inside our 
programs.  At this time, I would like to welcome principal of Barnard Elementary 
School Mrs. Melanie Morey to share.  
      Recommended Action:  This is a presentation for recognition only  
                                               no certificates will be handed out.

Autism Acceptance MonthAutism Acceptance Month

4
  Autism Spectrum Disorder (ASD) is a neurobiological 
disorder that typically affects development within the 
first three years of life and is characterized by deficits 
and symptoms in the following areas:
• Impaired communication ability (may be verbal, partially verbal, 
or non-verbal);
• Impaired social interaction;
• Restricted and/or repetitive interests/behaviors (includes 
repetitive actions; verbally repeating words, phrases, or 
sentences (i.e. perseverating on a topic)
• Sensory issues—over or under sensory stimulation

7
For Students:
• “Light it Up Blue” Day 
• Students will have the opportunity to have one fingernail 
painted blue. Each student will receive a small take home 
sheet explaining the purpose. The idea is to get others 
outside of the school asking students about their painted 
fingernail and students can talk to them about Autism 
acceptance. 
• Students will be encouraged to wear blue. 
• “Light it Up with Kindness” lessons for teachers to share with 
students throughout the week.
• Sensitivity training for general education peers
8
Autism Acceptance activities Autism Acceptance activities 
    For Staff:
• “Walk a Mile in our shoes”
• Staff are challenged to spend some time in an autism 
classroom  to better understand how to support students with 
special needs within their own classroom. 
• Staff kickoff breakfast- “Thanks for creating a culture of 
acceptance!”
• Building-wide professional development
• The spectrum of differentiation 
• Universal supports for ALL students

Autism Acceptance activities Autism Acceptance activities 
For the Community: 
• Autism Speaks Fundraiser: 
• Student council will be selling autism awareness bracelets 
throughout the month. 
• Proceeds will go towards the Autism Speaks walk this 
summer that families and staff are encouraged to 
participate in.

2. RECOGNITION 
B.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards 
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This 
evening we are proud to recognize the accomplishments of 39 individuals 
who were nominated for  the Troy School District Outstanding Teacher 
Award Program.  
The nominees represent exemplary educators at the elementary, middle 
and high school levels. They were nominated by their peers and by 
parents and t h i s  y e a r ,  w e  r e c e i v e d  m o r e  n o m i n a t i o n s  
t h a n  e v e r  t h a t  w e r e  s u b m i t t e d  by former and current 
students. These teachers have had a tremendous impact on the lives of 
so many through their dedication and commitment to their students. Their 
nomination letters each described how the professionals we honor here 
tonight go above and beyond to ensure that their students learn, grow 
and thrive.  
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Di stribute 
certificates/plants.) 
First, we honor 36 outstanding nominees, who have touched the lives of 
countless students and families in the Troy School District. We are grateful 
for their dedication and passion for student success.

Three of our district nominees were sent to Oakland Schools to represent 
Troy in the county-wide outstanding teacher award program, administered 
by Oakland Schools. 
Katy Stanley, Math and Social Studies Teacher at Larson Middle School, 
was named TSD Middle School Teacher of the Year. 
Stacie Klumpp, Math Teacher at Athens High School was named TSD 
High School Teacher of the Year.   
Ed Segovia, 2nd Grade Teacher at Morse Elementary was named TSD 
Elementary Teacher of the Year AND Overall TSD Teacher of the Year. 
At this time, I w ould like to invite Ms. Stanley, Mrs. Klumpp and Mr. 
Segovia to say a few words.

  President Schmidt called the meeting to order at 7:00 p.m.  
In  addition  to  Mr.  Schmidt,  present  were  Board  members 
Fleming, Gottlieb, Hammond, Hauff, and Philippart. Mr. Miletti

RECOGNITION  
Mission Moment 
TSD Students Reach Out to 
Marjory Stoneman Douglas 
at Troy High School, Athens High School, Boulan Park Middle 
School  and  Larson  Middle  School,  who  took  it  upon 
themselves to reach out in a show of support to students at 
Marjory  Stoneman  Douglas  High  School  in  Parkland,  Florida, 
t h e  s c e n e  o f  a  d e a d l y  s c h o o l  s h o o t i n g  l a s t  m o n t h .   A t  T r o y  
High and Boulan Park, our students, staff and teachers wrote 
hundreds of letters and cards to their counterparts in Florida,  
sending  messages  of  love,  prayers  and  positive  thoughts.  At 
Athens,  students  and  art  teachers  sent  student  artwork  and 
uplifting  messages.  And  at  Larson,  the  Craft  Club  made  a 
banner in a show of support that could be hung at the school 
to  let  Stoneman  Douglas  students  know  that  people  as  far 
away as Troy were thinking of them.  Though there is no way 
to take away the pain that students in Florida are feeling, we 
are proud that our students and staff took the initiative to be  
part of the healing process and decided to reach out and offer 
some  comfort.    Boulan  Park  M.S.  Teachers  Ann  O’Neill  and 
April  Reem  and  student  Britta  Pingree,  Larson  Teachers 
Amanda  Fisher,  Laura  Garver  and  Katy  Stanley,  Athens 
Teachers  Jennifer  McLellan  and  Melissa  Velk,  Troy  High 
Teacher  Jen  Opalewski  and  students  Emma  Bossenberger, 
Quinn Kelly, and Josiah Willis.   
16
High School Students 
Tonight, in our Mission Moment, we honor students and staff 
State School of Character – 
Larson Middle School 
Honorable Mention – Wass 
Elementary School 
We  also  honor  Larson  Middle  School  this  evening  for  being 
named a 2018 State School of Character.  A select few schools 
across the country are chosen each year as a State School of 
Character  through  the  implementation  of  11  core  principles, 
which  include  promoting  good  character,  culture  and  ethics; 
fostering  student  self‐motivation,  giving  students  the 
opportunity  for  moral  action  and  engaging  families  and 
community  members  as  partners  in  character‐building 
efforts. Larson Middle School was praised for the high quality 
of  work  in  all  11  principle  areas  and  the  school’s  application 
has  now  been  forwarded  on  for  consideration  as  a  National 
School  of  Character,  which  will  be  announced  later  this 
Spring.    We  would  also  like  to  commend  Wass  Elementary 
School, which was named a 2018 Honorable Mention School

Section 1 Property – Division 
WHEREAS, the School District is the owner of a certain parcel 
of  real  property  situated  in  the  City  of  Troy,  Oakland  County, 
Michigan, described as follows: 
    80  +/‐  acres  of  vacant  land,  commonly 
referred  to  as  Section  1  property,  Sidwell 
Numbers 20‐01‐300‐016 and 20‐01‐451‐001

5.        CONSENT
          F.    CONSIDERATION:       2017 - 18 Budget Amendment Resolution
Attached is the Budget Amendment Resolution for the 2017 - 18 fiscal year.  It includes the General 
Fund - Amendment 2, Food Service Fund - Amendment 1, Troy Career Center - Amendment 1, Adult 
Education and Community Service Fund - Amendment 1, Capital Projects Funds - Amendment 1, Capital 
Maintenance Fund - Amendment 1 and the budget adoption for the Early Childhood Learning Center 
Fund.  Approval of the attached resolution is recommended.
We do not have to read the Budget Amendment Resolution in its entirety.  However, it must be made a 
part of the official minutes.
RECOMMENDED ACTION:       Adopt the following resolution.
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION
Moved By:  ____________________
Supported By:  ____________________
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED, that the general appropriations and
budget of the Troy School District for the fiscal year 2017 - 18 for the
following funds be amended and approved as attached hereto and
made a part of the official minutes of the April 17, 2018 regular
meeting.

FST-AARC Charge
FST-AARC is to conduct a review of current Troy 
School District attendance area patterns according 
to specific criteria and present options to 
Superintendent of Schools, Dr. Richard Machesky.  
1. Resolve double split learning pathways in Troy.
2. Examine enrollment across buildings to address inappropriate 
overcrowding and inappropriate imbalances.
3. Minimize changes to school attendance areas across Troy.

Attendance Areas
Troy School District Primary School Paths
• Double Switch - Troy Union Elementary students split between Smith and Larson MS and 
the Smith MS students split again to Athens HS. 
• Single Switch – Wattles and Morse Elementary students attending Baker MS are split 
between Troy HS and Athens HS.
• Middle School Switch – Hamilton Elementary students split between Smith and Boulan 
Park MS and then they both go to Troy HS.
